● Found something concrete. manifests/chains/solana.yaml is in the repo and
  says:

  status: locale_only — FROST Ed25519 signer integration pending.
  Once signer is ready, promote to inbound_only, then bidirectional.
  signer: { protocol: frost, curve: ed25519, threshold: {t: 2, n: 3} }

  So:

  Yes — Solana is in scope. It's a planned chain with a defined promotion path
  (locale_only → inbound_only → bidirectional). Wrapping convention is
  documented (SOL → LSOL on the Lux side).

  But it's blocked upstream, not on the bridge. The manifest's own status field
  says "FROST Ed25519 signer integration pending." That's a cluster-side feature
  in mpcd — until mpcd ships FROST ed25519, the bridge has nothing to call. We
  can write all the assembler / broadcast / sig-parsing code we want; without a
  signer that produces ed25519 sigs, no Solana tx will ever land.

  What's actionable now

  - Confirm with whoever owns mpcd whether FROST ed25519 is on their near-term
  roadmap.
  - If yes, the bridge-side work (tx assembler for Solana, broadcast handler,
  ed25519 sig parsing dispatch in the signing/refund drivers) is ~2-3 days of
  focused work and can be done in parallel against a mock signer.
  - If FROST is months away, then for testnet maturity the highest-leverage work
  is still EVM-side: validate all EVM↔EVM pairs, add ERC-20 support, harden the
  rate-staleness path.

  Want me to check mpcd's repo / status for FROST progress before deciding
  scope?

● You're right to push me back to the canonical doc — I was overreaching.
  Re-reading REQUIREMENTS.md cleanly:

  Solana is NOT in the requirements doc.

  - §4 Glossary enumerates chains as b-chain, m-chain, t-chain, f-chain (not in
  scope v1), o-chain, r-chain — all internal Lux chains. No external chain
  (Ethereum, Solana, Base, etc.) is in that table.
  - §3 Non-Goals doesn't mention it either way.
  - §7–9 Phases prioritize: wire UI to live API → multi-tenant embed (Zoo) →
  publish @luxfi/bridge to npm. No cross-VM signing work is listed.
  - The only "frost" reference is BridgeMPCConfig.protocol accepting it as a
  string identifier the SDK can carry — not a guarantee any chain implements it.

  The manifests/chains/solana.yaml I cited earlier is a forward-looking sketch
  in the tree, not part of the canonical scope. I was conflating "exists in the
  repo" with "in the requirements."

  There's also a scope mismatch I should flag: REQUIREMENTS.md treats
  app/server/ (Express + Prisma) as the live API backend. The work we've been
  doing in cmd/bridge/ (Go binary, hanzoai/zip, MPC driver loop) isn't mentioned
  in the doc at all — my auto-memory says it's replacing app/server, but that
  replacement is not reflected in the canonical doc. Worth knowing whether:

  1. The Go bridge is an internal track that REQUIREMENTS.md just hasn't been
  updated for, or
  2. It's a parallel effort that should not be confused with the v1 deliverable.

  Either way — given REQUIREMENTS.md as the source of truth, the next thing
  isn't Solana. It's Phase 1.3 ("Run the SDK end-to-end against Lux testnet") +
  Phase 1.4 ("Deploy the Lux tenant"), then Phase 3.1 (compile SDK to dist/)
  which is the blocker tagged R1/R2/R6.

  How do you want to reconcile? Should REQUIREMENTS.md be updated to reflect the
  Go bridge work, or should I treat the Go binary as out-of-scope and focus on
  the documented Phase 1.3/3.1 items?

● Now I have a clear picture. Key findings:

  1. cmd/bridge/ is actively replacing app/server/ heavy paths. The main.go
  docstring says: "serves the bridge API natively for read paths (networks,
  tokens, quotes), and proxies MPC-heavy paths to the legacy Node backend during
  the migration to a fully native impl."
  2. The migration is well underway. What we've been building this session
  (swap_store, signing_driver, refund_driver, coingecko price feed, quote
  engine, MPC dispatch) is exactly the "fully native impl" that main.go's
  docstring describes as the target.
  3. Deployment integration lags the code. compose.testnet.yml still launches
  ghcr.io/luxfi/bridge-server:latest (Express). The Go binary has its own
  Dockerfile (cmd/bridge/Dockerfile → ghcr.io/luxfi/bridge:dev) but no compose
  file references it.
  4. REQUIREMENTS.md last touched at d5e82fb9 "chore: requirement updated" —
  predates the Go orchestration work in this session.

  So yes, the doc needs updating — but the right shape isn't obvious. Three
  reasonable directions:

      388  | Exchange SDK pattern (precedent) | `github.com/luxfi/exchang

      391 +## 13. Go bridge migration (`cmd/bridge`)                     
      392 +                                                              
      393 +The Node backend's heavy paths (swap orchestration, quote engi
          +ne, MPC dispatch, deposit watching) are being ported into a si
          +ngle Go binary at `cmd/bridge/` that also embeds the SPA via `
          +go:embed`. Goal: eliminate Express + Prisma + Postgres from th
          +e bridge core path and collapse the runtime to one image + one
          + config file. The migration is in progress on `whispers/bridge
          +v2` — the binary builds, the orchestrator drives real swaps ag
          +ainst testnet, but it is not yet wired into the production com
          +pose files.                                                   
      394 +                                                              
      395 +This section documents the Go binary as a parallel track. Phas
          +es 1–3 above continue to describe the TypeScript SDK + Express
          + backend deliverable, and remain canonical until cutover (§13.
          +6 step 5).                                                    
      396 +                                                              
      397 +### 13.1 Status (verified 2026-05-28 against working tree on `
          +whispers/bridgev2`)                                           
      398 +                                                              
      399 +| Component | State | Notes |                                 
      400 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      401 +| Networks / tokens / limits / exchanges | ✅ native | Served 
          +from YAML (`cmd/bridge/networks.testnet.yaml`, `networks.examp
          +le.yaml`). No DB. |                                           
      402 +| Quote engine | ✅ native | `quote_engine.go` + `coingecko_pr
          +ice_feed.go`. CoinGecko primary + static fallback (LUX/ZOO liv
          +e in static permanently — neither is listed on CoinGecko). Rec
          +eive amount is snapshot-stamped on the `Swap` row at create ti
          +me. |                                                         
      403 +| Swap store | ✅ native | `swap_store.go` (in-memory map; `za
          +p_store.go` is the Hanzo Base / SQLite-embedded backing, not y
          +et the default). Status set: `user_deposit_pending → bridge_tr
          +ansfer_pending → broadcasting → completed`, plus `refund_pendi
          +ng` (stale-quote handoff) and `refunding` (legacy insufficient
          +-funds path). |                                               
      404 +| Deposit watcher | ✅ native | `deposit_watcher.go`, 15s poll
          + loop. Advances `user_deposit_pending → bridge_transfer_pendin
          +g_signing` once the source-chain deposit confirms. |          
      405 +| Signing driver | ✅ native | `signing_driver.go`. Drives `br
          +idge_transfer_pending_signing` → MPC sign → `broadcasting`. Qu
          +ote-staleness guard via `--quote-max-age` (default 30 m) — sta
          +le swaps are kicked to the refund driver so the user gets thei
          +r deposit back rather than executing at a drifted rate. |     
      406 +| Refund driver | ✅ native | `refund_driver.go`. Handles both
          + legacy insufficient-funds refunds *and* stale-quote handoffs 
          +(`refund_pending`). Builds + MPC-signs + broadcasts the refund
          + tx. |                                                        
      407 +| MPC dispatch | ✅ native | Calls `mpcd` directly over HTTP (
          +`/keygen`, `/sign`) via `--mpc-url`. Per-swap deposit wallet (
          +created lazily on first quote) plus a long-lived release walle
          +t per destination network. No intermediate Node MPC service. |
      408 +| Explorer / settings / auth | 🟡 proxied | Falls back to `app
          +/server` via `--backend`. |                                   
      409 +| Storage durability | 🟡 in-memory | Default store is `swap_s
          +tore.go` (process-local map). `zap_store.go` SQLite backing ex
          +ists but is not the default — a binary restart drops in-flight
          + swaps. |                                                     
      410 +| Deployment | 🟡 dev only | `cmd/bridge/Dockerfile` builds `g
          +hcr.io/luxfi/bridge:dev`. No `compose.*.yml` or `k8s/` manifes
          +t references the Go binary yet. `compose.testnet.yml` still la
          +unches `ghcr.io/luxfi/bridge-server:latest` (Express) + `ghcr.
          +io/luxfi/bridge-ui:latest`. |                                 
      411 +                                                              
      412 +### 13.2 Tech stack                                           
      413 +                                                              
      414 +- **HTTP framework**: `github.com/hanzoai/zip` (Sinatra-style 
          +on Fiber v3 / fasthttp). New handlers must use `zip.Ctx`; do n
          +ot introduce stdlib `net/http` handlers on the request path.  
      415 +- **Logging**: `github.com/luxfi/log` (`luxlog`). No `slog`, n
          +o direct `zap`.                                               
      416 +- **Storage**: Hanzo Base (SQLite-embedded via `zap_store.go`)
          + is the target backing; today the default is the in-memory map
          + in `swap_store.go`.                                          
      417 +- **MPC**: HTTP calls to `mpcd`. The cluster enforces 2-of-3 t
          +hreshold and is authenticated via bearer token (`--mpc-token`)
          + — for local dev the token can be derived from a node identity
          + file via `SHA-256(seed ‖ "mpc-internal-api")`.               
      418 +- **Pricing**: CoinGecko `simple/price` API with 30 s TTL cach
          +e + single-flight batched fetches. `FallbackFeed` composite fa
          +lls through to a static feed for assets CoinGecko does not lis
          +t (LUX, ZOO).                                                 
      419 +                                                              
      420 +### 13.3 Architectural conventions                            
      421 +                                                              
      422 +- **Deposit wallet vs release wallet split.** Per-swap MPC wal
          +lets receive deposits; one long-lived release wallet per desti
          +nation network pays out settlements from operator-funded liqui
          +dity. Refunds flow back from the deposit wallet, not the relea
          +se wallet.                                                    
      423 +- **MPC-only, no teleporter.** Every swap goes through `create
          +MPCWalletForDeposit`; the legacy teleporter contracts in `tele
          +port.ts` are off the happy path on this branch.               
      424 +- **Quote locked at create time.** `Swap.ReceiveAmount` is sta
          +mped on the row when the user creates the swap, then enforced 
          +at signing time via `--quote-max-age`. Stale quotes never exec
          +ute at the new price; they refund.                            
      425 +                                                              
      426 +### 13.4 Configuration surface (selected flags)               
      427 +                                                              
      428 +| Flag | Purpose |                                            
      429 +|---|---|                                                     
      430 +| `--config` | Networks/tokens/limits YAML path. |            
      431 +| `--backend` | Legacy Node backend URL for the still-proxied 
          +paths (`/explorer/*`, `/settings`). |                         
      432 +| `--mpc-url` | `mpcd` keygen + sign endpoint. Required when S
          +DK requests carry `use_deposit_address=true`. |               
      433 +| `--mpc-token` / `--mpc-identity-file` | Bearer auth for `mpc
          +d`. Identity file derives the token deterministically — conven
          +ience for local dev; prod sets the token explicitly. |        
      434 +| `--source-rpc-overrides` | Per-network RPC overrides for the
          + deposit watcher and `/v1/bridge/check-deposit` (e.g. `ETHEREU
          +M_SEPOLIA=https://ethereum-sepolia-rpc.publicnode.com`). |    
      435 +| `--coingecko` (+ `--coingecko-api-key`, `--coingecko-cache-t
          +tl`, `--coingecko-timeout`) | Layer CoinGecko in front of the 
          +static feed. Default off. |                                   
      436 +| `--quote-max-age` | Max age (default 30 m) of a create-time 
          +quote before the signing driver refuses to sign and hands off 
          +to the refund driver. Zero disables — only safe for stablecoin
          +-only deployments. |                                          
      437 +| `--disable-deposit-watcher` / `--disable-signing-driver` | D
          +isable background loops (testing / manual operation). |       
      438 +                                                              
      439 +### 13.5 Current testnet scope                                
      440 +                                                              
      441 +`cmd/bridge/networks.testnet.yaml` ships **4 EVM testnet chain
          +s**: `ETHEREUM_SEPOLIA`, `LUX_TESTNET`, `BASE_SEPOLIA`, `HOLES
          +KY_TESTNET`, with native gas tokens only (ETH, LUX). No ERC-20
          +s yet. End-to-end signed swaps are working for Sepolia → LUX a
          +s of 2026-05-28.                                              
      442 +                                                              
      443 +This is narrower than what `app/bridge/TESTNET-E2E.md` §2 asse
          +rts the chain picker should show ("at least: … Solana Devnet, 
          +Bitcoin Testnet, Ton Testnet, XRP Testnet, BSC Testnet, Zoo Te
          +stnet"). TESTNET-E2E.md is written against the Express backend
          + at `bridge-api.lux.network`; the Go binary's chain set is int
          +entionally smaller while EVM↔EVM is being hardened. Reconcilia
          +tion happens at cutover (§13.6 step 5).                       
      444 +                                                              
      445 +### 13.6 Cutover plan                                         
      446 +                                                              
      447 +| # | Step | Acceptance |                                     
      448 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      449 +| 1 | EVM↔EVM hardening | All 4-chain pairs in `networks.testn
          +et.yaml` complete a signed round-trip end-to-end against the G
          +o binary. Today only Sepolia → LUX is exercised. |            
      450 +| 2 | Persistence default | `zap_store.go` (SQLite) promoted t
          +o the default store so binary restarts don't drop in-flight sw
          +aps. |                                                        
      451 +| 3 | ERC-20 path | `txassembler` + `networks.testnet.yaml` ex
          +tended for at least Sepolia USDC. |                           
      452 +| 4 | Compose wiring | `bridge-go` service added to `compose.t
          +estnet.yml` alongside `bridge-server`. SPA continues to talk t
          +o `bridge-server` while the Go binary soaks. |                
      453 +| 5 | DNS cutover | After a 1-week soak with no regressions, `
          +bridge-api.lux.network` repoints to `ghcr.io/luxfi/bridge`. `b
          +ridge-server` image retired. |                                
      454 +| 6 | Non-EVM (Solana, BTC, TON) | Bridge-side `txassembler` +
          + `broadcast` family dispatch implemented **only after** `mpcd`
          + ships FROST Ed25519 (and Schnorr for BTC). See `manifests/cha
          +ins/solana.yaml` for the planned promotion path (`locale_only 
          +→ inbound_only → bidirectional`). |                           
      455 +                                                              
      456 +### 13.7 Known gaps                                           
      457 +                                                              
      458 +| # | Gap | Impact |                                          
      459 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      460 +| G1 | `broadcast/client.go` returns `ErrFamilyNotImplemented`
          + for every non-EVM chain family. Signature parsing assumes ECD
          +SA `R∥S∥V`. | Blocks Bitcoin, TON, XRP, Solana even once `mpcd
          +` ships the additional signature curves. |                    
      461 +| G2 | Only native gas tokens in `networks.testnet.yaml`. | No
          + ERC-20 (USDC/USDT) testnet path yet. |                       
      462 +| G3 | `cmd/bridge` not referenced from any `compose.*.yml` or
          + `k8s/` manifest. | Production deploy targets are still the Ex
          +press + bridge-ui pair. |                                     
      463 +| G4 | Default store is in-memory (`swap_store.go`). | Binary 
          +restart drops in-flight swaps. |                              
      464 +| G5 | `app/bridge/TESTNET-E2E.md` Phase 1.3 sign-off runs aga
          +inst `bridge-api.lux.network` (Express). | Closing Phase 1.3 d
          +oes not automatically validate the Go binary; the Go binary ne
          +eds its own sign-off doc. |                                   
      465 +                                                              
      466 +### 13.8 Open questions for sponsor                           
      467 +                                                              
      468 +1. Is the Go binary expected to replace `app/server/` *entirel
          +y*, or only the heavy paths (swap / quote / MPC) while Express
          + continues to host explorer + settings + auth long-term?      
      469 +2. Acceptance bar for the testnet soak in §13.6 step 4 — numbe
          +r of completed swaps, span in days, failure-rate ceiling.     
      470 +3. Mainnet cutover timing: tied to a specific Phase 3 mileston
          +e (e.g. SDK publication), or independent?                     
      471 +4. Does §3 Non-Goals ("Rewriting `app/server/` routes, schema,

          +s by design?

❯ So regarding to check about REQUIREMENTS.md, what is the next top priority?   

● Reading the doc with done/pending markers explicit, the highest-leverage next
  thing is §13.6 step 1 — finish the EVM↔EVM matrix on the Go binary. Reasoning:

  Why this beats the alternatives

  - It's the only remaining 🟡 in the Go-binary track. Everything else in §13.6
  is ⬜ and depends on this being closed first (you can't promote storage, soak
  in compose, or DNS-cut over until the orchestrator handles all chain pairs).
  - Sepolia → LUX is 1 of 7 directions. The other 6 — Sepolia↔Base Sepolia,
  Sepolia↔Holesky, Base Sepolia↔LUX, Holesky↔LUX, LUX→Sepolia, LUX→{Base
  Sepolia, Holesky} — exercise the same code paths but on different chain
  configs, so each completed direction is high-confidence per-unit work. Most
  failures will be config gaps (RPC override, gas estimation, address
  formatting), not orchestrator bugs.
  - It directly validates everything we shipped this session (CoinGecko pricing,
  quote-at-create, staleness check, MPC deposit wallet, refund driver) under a
  wider set of conditions.
  - No external blocker. Compare to:
    - Phase 1.3 (Express path) — needs the deployed bridge-api.lux.network stack
  and a TESTNET-E2E.md walkthrough; separate gate from the Go work.
    - Phase 3.1 (SDK dist/ build) — unrelated workstream, blocks npm publish but
  not bridge functionality.
    - §13 step 6 (Solana) — blocked on mpcd FROST Ed25519, nothing actionable
  from this side.
    - ERC-20 path (§13 step 3) — high-value but needs txassembler work; better
  to land it after the orchestrator is proven on all 4 EVM chains.

  What "done" looks like

  A 7×1 results table: each direction in the matrix completes a signed swap,
  balance lands on destination, no manual intervention. Probably 1–2 days of
  focused testing, with the most likely failure modes being:
  - BASE_SEPOLIA / HOLESKY gas estimation if the release wallet doesn't have
  native funds there
  - RPC rate limiting on the default endpoints (would need
  --source-rpc-overrides)
  - Reverse direction (LUX → X) needing a release wallet on Sepolia/Base 
  Sepolia/Holesky, funded with that chain's native token

  Worth doing in parallel, low-cost

  Get sponsor answers on §13.8 — especially Q1 (is the Go binary replacing
  app/server entirely or only heavy paths?). That answer changes whether ERC-20
  / explorer / auth need to be ported next, or stay on Express forever.

  Want me to start running the matrix and produce a results table?

● Next priority: §13.6 step 3 — ERC-20 path (Sepolia USDC)

  Why this beats everything else right now:

  - Not faucet-blocked. You already have 0.46 Sepolia ETH for gas, and Circle's
  Sepolia USDC faucet (https://faucet.circle.com/) has been the most reliable
  testnet faucet in the ecosystem for years — no Alchemy/mainnet-ETH gate.
  - Highest user-visible value of any remaining work. Mainnet USDC bridging is
  the canonical bridge use case. Without ERC-20 support the binary can't even
  pretend to serve real flows. Native-only is a toy.
  - It exercises new orchestrator code paths. Today every swap is a native-token
  transfer (value field set on the dest tx, no calldata). USDC needs:
    - Source-side: detect ERC-20 deposit by watching
  Transfer(from→deposit_address, value) event logs, not just balance deltas
    - Tx assembler: build an ERC-20 transfer(to, amount) calldata for the
  release tx, with a different gas estimation profile (~50k gas vs 21k)
    - Quote engine: USDC has 6 decimals — needs to handle non-18-decimal
  correctly (current path assumes 18)
  - No mpcd changes needed. Still ECDSA signing on EVM. The signer dispatch path
  is unchanged.
  - Closes §13.7 G2 in the same stroke.

  Estimated shape of the work (~3–4 hours):
  1. Add USDC_SEPOLIA token entry in cmd/bridge/networks.testnet.yaml (contract
  0x1c7D4B196Cb0C7B01d743Fbc6116a902379C7238, 6 decimals)
  2. Extend deposit_watcher.go to subscribe/poll for ERC-20 Transfer logs in
  addition to native balance checks
  3. Extend txassembler to build 0xa9059cbb (ERC-20 transfer) calldata when
  destination asset is ERC-20
  4. Adjust gas-estimation defaults (21000 → ~65000) for ERC-20 release txes
  5. Quote engine: confirm decimals handling — CoinGecko returns USD per token,
  but the bridge needs to convert against 10^6 not 10^18 for amounts

  Alternative if you'd rather defer ERC-20: §13.6 step 2 (promote zap_store.go
  SQLite backing to the default store). Smaller scope (~1 hour), lower leverage.
  The current in-memory map is acceptable for testnet but the stuck-swap
  residue we saw earlier (8342 retries) would survive a restart with persistence
  — which is sometimes good, sometimes bad. Lower priority unless you want it
  as a quick win before tackling ERC-20.

  Worth doing in parallel, cheap: answer §13.8 Q1 yourself — does the Go binary
  replace app/server entirely, or only heavy paths? That answer shapes whether
  ERC-20 (and explorer, settings, auth) all need Go ports, or only
  swap/quote/MPC. If "entirely", we have a lot more porting ahead than §13
  currently scopes.

  Want to start on ERC-20 (Sepolia USDC)?

● Found it. "ZAP" has two distinct meanings in this ecosystem:
  1. luxfi/zapdb — storage library (used by cmd/bridge/zap_store.go)
  2. luxfi/node/consensus/zap + luxfi/node/vms/rpcchainvm/zap — a Lux-native
  interface to the node

  The boss's "native ZAP interface to the lux node" clearly refers to #2. Let me
  investigate what it actually is and whether the bridge uses it.

  Listed 1 directory (ctrl+o to expand)

● Direct answer: No, the boss's instruction has not been finished. Three
  sub-pieces, only one is done:

  Boss said: "make a api.bridge server if necessary to proxy ... deploy as
    centralized helper"
  Status: ✅ done
  Evidence: cmd/bridge is exactly this — single Go binary, embeds SPA, serves
  the
    /v1/bridge/* surface, can proxy /explorer//settings via --backend.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Boss said: "point to the b-chain ... that is the new bridge API"
  Status: ❌ not enabled
  Evidence: --bchain-url is optional and defaults empty. The running binary
    reports bchain_rpc: false in /health. No live swap touches BridgeVM.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Boss said: "uses the native ZAP interface to the lux node"
  Status: ❌ not started
  Evidence: Zero imports of luxfi/node/consensus/zap (or any luxfi/*/zap
  package)
    anywhere under cmd/bridge/ or internal/. The existing
    internal/bchain/client.go is plain HTTP JSON-RPC against
    ${node}/ext/bc/B/rpc, written as a Go port of the speculative bridge-rpc.ts
  —
     not the ZAP protocol the boss specified.

  What ZAP actually is (per /root/luxify/node/consensus/zap/bridge.go:4-6):

  ▎ "Package zap provides ZAP (Zero-copy Agent Protocol) integration for Lux 
  ▎ consensus. This package bridges ZAP's agentic consensus with Lux's Quasar 
  ▎ threshold signatures, enabling W3C DID-based validator identity and 
  ▎ post-quantum secure finality."

  So it's a substantially different interface than the JSON-RPC the current
  internal/bchain uses:
  - Zero-copy semantics (likely binary framing, not JSON)
  - DID-based validator identity (W3C DID, did:lux:… from did.go)
  - Quasar threshold signatures (the post-quantum signer flow)
  - Designed for agentic consensus — i.e. it's how clients participate in the
  node's consensus protocol, not just query it

  Why the existing internal/bchain doesn't count, per the auto-memory I already 
  had:

  The old memory [[architecture-go-bridge-stack]] flagged this as a partial
  state 2 days ago:

  ▎ Phase 4.2 — Go b-chain client: ⚠️ partial — Built against speculative TS 
  ▎ spec; methods don't match real BridgeVM. Refocus to LP-333 signer-set 
  ▎ methods is the proper Phase 4.2.

  That memory was written BEFORE the boss directive about ZAP was surfaced to
  me. The directive escalates "refocus" to a hard requirement.

  What's missing concretely

  1. A Go consumer of luxfi/node/consensus/zap (or whatever its public client
  API is — bridge.go looks server-side; need to check whether there's a client
  SDK at node/network/zap/ or similar).
  2. A replacement for internal/bchain/client.go that:
    - Uses ZAP framing instead of JSON-RPC
    - Calls the real LP-333 BridgeVM surface (bridge_getSignerSetInfo,
  bridge_getCurrentEpoch, etc.) for signer-set + epoch queries
    - Carries a did:lux:… identity for the bridge as a participating client
  3. Wiring in main.go so the bridge can't not talk to b-chain — --bchain-url
  should become required, not optional, for production profiles.
  4. REQUIREMENTS.md §13 update — the cutover plan currently treats cmd/bridge
  as a standalone API replacement; per the boss it should be a centralized
  helper proxy to b-chain, which is a meaningfully different architecture.

  My recommendation for next priority shift

  This is bigger than the EVM matrix or ERC-20 work and changes the
  architecture. Before writing any code I'd want to:

  1. Confirm with the boss that the ZAP-client surface is published / stable for
  consumers — node/consensus/zap looks server-side (it's a bridge between ZAP
  and Quasar inside the node). The client-side ZAP entry point may live in a
  different package (node/network/zap?, an external SDK?). One quick directory
  scan would clarify.
  2. Confirm whether the LP-333 BridgeVM methods we want to query are reachable
  via ZAP, or whether ZAP is consensus-participation-only and LP-333 stays on
  JSON-RPC.
  3. If both check out, refactor internal/bchain to be a ZAP client.

  Want me to do that recon next — figure out what the ZAP client surface looks
  like and how a Go consumer is supposed to use it — before changing any
  priorities in REQUIREMENTS.md?

● Recon complete. Here's the structured picture.

  What ZAP actually is

  ZAP = Zero-copy Agent Protocol. Lux's internal binary RPC transport, designed
  to replace gRPC for plugin IPC and high-performance internal control planes.
  Three-layered stack:

  Layer: Wire
  Module: github.com/luxfi/zap@v0.2.0
  Purpose: TCP framing, opcodes, message envelopes
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Layer: Convenience
  Module: github.com/luxfi/api/zap (imported as zapwire)
  Purpose: Dial / Conn / Listen helpers
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Layer: Session
  Module: github.com/luxfi/mpc/pkg/zap
  Purpose: App-layer X25519+ML-KEM-768 PQ handshake → AES-256-GCM AEAD per
  LP-022
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Layer: Auth
  Module: github.com/luxfi/mpc/pkg/zapauth
  Purpose: JWKS-validated bearer tokens before handshake, per LP-103

  Plus a separate node/consensus/zap package which is consensus-side — bridges
  ZAP-style agentic consensus with Quasar threshold sigs (W3C DIDs, etc.).
  Different scope from the wire-transport layer above; not what a client dials.

  Where ZAP is wired today

  Servers:
  - node/vms/rpcchainvm/zap/server.go — VM plugin protocol (the node ↔ subnet VM
  bridge, replacing the gRPC plugin protocol)
  - node/db/rpcdb/zap_server.go — DB plugin protocol
  - mpc/pkg/api/zap_kms_server.go — KMS control plane (KMS dials MPC daemon)

  Clients (canonical patterns to copy):
  - node/vms/rpcchainvm/zap/client.go — node engine talking to VM plugins
  (Initialize, BuildBlock, etc.)
  - mpcd's KMS server-side usage shows the listener pattern (StartKMSZAPWith at
  mpc/cmd/mpcd/main.go:962)

  The blocker

  BridgeVM does not have a ZAP listener. Grepping /root/luxify/chains/bridgevm/
  for any zap import returns nothing — its files are rpc.go (JSON-RPC),
  block.go, codec.go, mpc.go, bridge_signing.go, etc. The only way to call
  BridgeVM externally today is JSON-RPC at ${node}/ext/bc/B/rpc.

  No public ZAP listener exists on the node at all. Every zapwire.Listen call I
  found is bound to either:
  - A plugin subprocess socket (rpcchainvm/zap, rpcdb/zap), or
  - An internal control-plane address (mpcd KMS ZAP)

  There's no equivalent of ${node}/ext/bc/B/zap (or any wire-port equivalent)
  for external clients to dial.

  What this means for the boss's directive

  Strict interpretation — "the bridge talks to b-chain via ZAP" — is currently 
  impossible. The wire library, session/auth packages, and reference client
  patterns all exist, but BridgeVM hasn't published a ZAP surface. The bridge
  can't dial something that isn't listening.

  Three realistic paths forward:

  Path: A. Hold + refocus
  What it requires: Stay on JSON-RPC internal/bchain, but actually wire it to
  the
    real LP-333 surface (bridge_getSignerSetInfo, bridge_getCurrentEpoch, etc.)
    and turn on --bchain-url in the running config.
  Pros: Achievable today. Closes the "bridge isn't even talking to b-chain" gap.
  Cons: Doesn't satisfy the ZAP part of the directive.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Path: B. Push BridgeVM to expose ZAP
  What it requires: Coordinate with the node/chains team to add a
    chains/bridgevm/zap_server.go exposing LP-333 methods as ZAP opcodes. Mirror

    the pattern from mpc/pkg/api/zap_kms_server.go.
  Pros: Strict satisfaction of the directive.
  Cons: Out of the bridge repo's control. Could take weeks.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Path: C. Wrap mpcd ZAP
  What it requires: If the methods we actually need (signer set, epoch,
  threshold
    pubkey) are all queryable via mpcd's existing KMS ZAP server, we could
    ZAP-to-mpcd and skip BridgeVM.
  Pros: Uses an existing listener.
  Cons: Doesn't match what the boss said ("ZAP interface to the lux node"
  implies
    the node, not mpcd).

  My recommendation: A first, then B in parallel. Path A unblocks the bridge's
  b-chain integration immediately — it's strictly required regardless (the
  running binary has bchain_rpc: false), and the auto-memory already flagged
  "refocus to LP-333 signer-set methods" 2 days ago. Path B is the conversation
  to start with whoever owns chains/bridgevm/: "are there plans to add a ZAP
  listener? Here's our pattern to mirror." Doing A doesn't block B; the JSON-RPC
  client can be swapped for a ZAP client later without API surface changes.

  One additional finding worth flagging: the existing auto-memory
  [[architecture-go-bridge-stack]] says Phase 4.2 b-chain client is ⚠️ partial
  because it was built against the speculative TS spec. That's still accurate —
  and now I can be more specific about why: the methods it implements don't
  exist on real BridgeVM, AND even if they did, the transport (JSON-RPC vs ZAP)
  is the wrong long-term layer.

  Want me to either (1) update REQUIREMENTS.md §13 with this recon finding so
  the priority is captured, (2) start the Path A refocus (wire internal/bchain
  to the real LP-333 surface), or (3) write the conversation-starter doc for the
  node team to add a BridgeVM ZAP listener?

  2. MetaMask should switch to Lux Testnet (96368) — and if you haven't added it
  yet, MetaMask will prompt to add it (RPC
  https://api.lux-test.network/ext/bc/C/rpc, symbol LUX, explorer
  https://explore.lux-test.network — all baked into the defineChain block)
  3. Then MetaMask prompts to confirm sending 10 LUX to the deposit address
  4. Confirm — the bridge detects within ~15s, MPC signs, broadcasts to Sepolia,
  your wallet receives ~0.0122 ETH

  If the chain-switch prompt asks you to add the network, click "Approve" — that
  just records Lux Testnet in your MetaMask. The subsequent send-tx prompt is
  the actual deposit.

  Let me know what MetaMask shows when you click Bridge.

● Two important findings:

  1. All 5 EVM testnets work end-to-end (quote + ERC-20)
  2. Non-EVM swap creation is mixed:
    - BTC: mpcd returns a mainnet-format address (1Ld2... starts with 1 =
  mainnet P2PKH). Testnet BTC addresses start with m/n/tb1. The address encoder
  doesn't honor the testnet flag.
    - SOL: explicit failure — no sol address returned from MPC keygen (confirms
  mpcd lacks ed25519 / FROST per the §13.6 G1 gap)

  Let me check TON and quickly verify the BTC mainnet/testnet encoding gap.

● Bash(echo "=== TON Testnet → LUX swap creation ==="
      curl -s -X POST -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{…)  ⎿  === TON Testnet → LUX swap creation ===
     {
         "detail": "no ton address returned from MPC keygen",
     … +42 lines (ctrl+o to expand)

● Empirical results confirm the §13.7 G1 gap is broader than expected:

  Non-EVM: BTC
  mpcd keygen: Returns address but mainnet format (1Ld2...) for testnet —
    dangerous, users could send mainnet BTC
  Quote: ✓
  Verdict: Address-encoder bug; cluster needs testnet flag handling
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Non-EVM: SOL
  mpcd keygen: no sol address returned from MPC keygen
  Quote: ✓
  Verdict: mpcd lacks ed25519 / FROST
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Non-EVM: TON
  mpcd keygen: no ton address returned from MPC keygen
  Quote: ✓
  Verdict: Same (TON shares SOL keygen slot per mchain/client.go:590-594)

  Two SOL/TON self-gate at create-time (clean failure). BTC is the dangerous one
  — it returns a valid-looking but mainnet-format address. If a user sent real
  testnet BTC there, the bridge couldn't detect it (Blockstream testnet API
  would say balance=0), and if they sent mainnet BTC they'd lose funds.

  Safer path: disable deposit on all three non-EVM until the mpcd + encoder gaps
  close. Keep them in registry so the picker can show them as "coming soon" but
  can't be selected.

  Empirical findings worth flagging:
  1. BTC bug is the surprise — I expected mpcd to either return a testnet
  address or fail cleanly. Instead it returns a mainnet address, which is the
  worst possible failure mode (user sees a valid-looking address, sends funds,
  loses them). This is now documented in §13.7 G1.
  2. The mpcd ed25519 gap blocks both Solana AND TON (same keygen slot in
  mchain/client.go:590-594). So shipping ed25519 unblocks two chains, not one.

  REQUIREMENTS.md updated: §13.5 now lists all 12 networks with per-chain
  status; §13.7 G1 specifies the 4 distinct layers of non-EVM brokenness; §13.7
  G2 closed for testnet ERC-20.

  Want me to write a small bridge-side BTC mainnet→testnet re-encoder (decode
  the base58check P2PKH, swap version byte 0x00 → 0x6f, re-encode), or is that
  better as a follow-up? The encoder is ~30 lines of Go and would unlock
  BITCOIN_TESTNET deposit-only flow without waiting for mpcd. Solana/TON still
  need mpcd FROST regardless.

          +ature parsing assumes ECDSA `R∥S∥V`; (c) mpcd keygen returns `
          +"no sol address returned from MPC keygen"` / same for TON — ed
          +25519 + FROST not wired in the cluster, blocks both deposit an
          +d withdrawal for SOL/TON; (d) ~~BTC mainnet-format address ret
          +urned for testnet~~ **fixed in-bridge 2026-05-28** via `intern
          +al/mchain/btc_address.go` (base58check re-encoder with version
          +-byte swap; 11 unit tests; empirically verified against Blocks
          +tream testnet API). BTC now enabled source-only. | Withdrawal 
          +to non-EVM still gated by (a) + (b). SOL/TON deposit still gat
          +ed by (c). BTC deposit unblocked. |

  mainnet→testnet conversion via the actual mpcd-empirical address (1Ld2… →
  mnMH…), idempotence on testnet input, P2SH support (defensive), bech32 testnet
  passthrough, bech32 mainnet error, bad-checksum detection, empty-input guard,
  leading-zero/leading-1 preservation in base58, invalid-char rejection,

  REQUIREMENTS.md updated: §13.5 BTC row flipped from ⬜ to 🟡 source-only with
  the encoder reference; §13.7 G1 now correctly attributes the remaining
  blockers (withdrawal still gated by broadcast/client.go + signing curve gaps;
  SOL/TON deposit still gated by mpcd ed25519). The "BTC mainnet-format" gap
  that I called out as "dangerous" earlier is closed.

  What BTC source-only unlocks: BTC testnet → LUX swaps. A user with testnet BTC
  (faucet at https://coinfaucet.eu/en/btc-testnet/ or
  https://bitcoinfaucet.uo1.net/) can deposit to the freshly-derived address;
  the deposit watcher polls Blockstream testnet, MPC signs a LUX release tx,
  user receives LUX. Settlement on Bitcoin (LUX → BTC) remains blocked.

✻ Crunched for 7m 21s

❯ what's the next priority?                                                     

● Looking at the doc, the engineering side of Phase 1.4 is done — what's left is
  operator-side (Secret, apply, DNS, fund release wallets). The remaining
  engineering items are smaller and more tactical. Ranked by leverage:

  1. Mainnet ConfigMap parity (recommended)

  The k8s manifest at k8s/bridge-deployment.yaml has its ConfigMap inlined with
  only 4 mainnet chains: LUX, Ethereum, Base, BSC. We just expanded testnet to 9
  EVM + 1 non-EVM. When the operator does kubectl apply, mainnet ships with a
  smaller chain set than testnet — which is backwards (testnet should be a
  subset of mainnet, not the reverse).

  Concrete work: add ARBITRUM_MAINNET (42161), OPTIMISM_MAINNET (10),
  POLYGON_MAINNET (137), AVALANCHE_MAINNET (43114), plus their USDC contracts
  (all in internal/tokens/tokens.go already, just not in the ConfigMap YAML).
  Optionally BITCOIN_MAINNET source-only — mpcd's mainnet keygen returns correct
  mainnet addresses, so no re-encoder needed there. ~15 min of YAML editing for
  4–5 more chains live on mainnet day-one.

  Why it's the top item: this is the last engineering gap blocking production
  deploy. After it lands, the operator handoff is truly the only thing
  remaining.

  2. compose.mainnet.yml bridge-go service (Recommended after #1)

  Symmetric with the testnet compose work I did earlier. Right now
  compose.mainnet.yml only has bridge-server (Express); adding the Go binary
  alongside lets operators stage the mainnet stack locally before production
  deploy. ~10 min — copy the testnet pattern, swap to mainnet RPCs.

  3. Go-binary-specific Phase 1.3 sign-off doc (closes §13.7 G5)

  app/bridge/TESTNET-E2E.md is written against the Express backend at
  bridge-api.lux.network. The Go binary path doesn't have an equivalent sign-off
  doc — meaning Phase 1.3 can't formally close on the Go binary path. Write
  docs/testnet-e2e-go-binary.md that's the same walkthrough but against the new
  stack. ~30 min.

  4. ERC-20 USDC live validation (closes §13.6 step 3)

  Get USDC on Sepolia from Circle's faucet (https://faucet.circle.com — no
  auth-gating), deposit on a Sepolia USDC → LUX swap, watch the full pipeline
  complete. Closes the row from 🟡 to ✅. ~5 min after the faucet drop.

  5. Mainnet release wallet provisioning runbook (closes the last operator
  unknown)

  The deploy runbook I wrote tells operators what needs to happen for release
  wallets but not how. Add a section: "After first swap per destination network,
  the bridge auto-mints a release wallet via mpcd. Get the address via kubectl 
  exec ... cat /var/lib/lux-bridge/release-wallets.json, then fund from treasury
  with ~0.5 native gas per chain."

  Lower priority (blocked on upstream):
  - Solana/TON deposit (waiting on mpcd FROST Ed25519)
  - Cross-EVM matrix (waiting on faucets)
  - BridgeVM ZAP listener (cross-team work)

  My recommendation: do #1 next. It's the cleanest engineering item that
  materially advances production readiness, and after it the project is
  engineering-complete for the live path — everything else is either ops handoff
  or upstream-blocked work.

  Want to go with mainnet ConfigMap parity?

  Pre-existing bug fixed: ConfigMap was using lux-mainnet/ethereum-mainnet/etc.
  — internal lookups in internal/mchain, internal/depositcheck,
  internal/broadcast, internal/txassembler, internal/tokens all key off
  uppercase-underscore convention. As shipped, that ConfigMap would have caused
  every swap to fail at deposit detection. Now matches the testnet convention.

  - Test bridge instance loads it and serves /v1/bridge/networks returning all
  11 networks with correct token counts per network
  - Sample quote works: 100 USDC (Ethereum) → 40 LUX (Lux) at expected pricing

  Notable choices captured in YAML comments:
  - BSC USDC/USDT use 18 decimals (BSC convention) — wrong decimals here would
  credit users with 10^12× the expected amount; called out so future edits don't
  mis-copy
  - Polygon native ticker uses MATIC to match internal/tokens/tokens.go; Polygon
  ecosystem rebranded to POL in late 2024 — flagged as a known inconsistency to
  clean up in a follow-up
  - BTC source-only mainnet — mpcd's mainnet keygen returns correct addresses,
  so the testnet re-encoder is a no-op on mainnet; settlement still requires BTC
  tx assembler + Schnorr

● The empirical audit reveals a meaningful gap I hadn't appreciated: the 
  README's "key features" mostly live in app/server (Express, being retired), 
  not in cmd/bridge (Go, what's going live).

  Feature audit — actual code, not docs

  README Feature: MPC 2-of-3 (classical)
  app/server (Express): ✅
  cmd/bridge (Go): ✅
  Empirical evidence: /health reports mpc_keygen: true; 2 swaps signed this
    session
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  README Feature: PQ-safe lattice (pulsar/corona/magnetar)
  app/server (Express): declared in BridgeMPCConfig.protocol
  cmd/bridge (Go): ❌
  Empirical evidence: /health.profile = BRIDGE_CLASSICAL_COMPAT_UNSAFE,
    post_quantum_end_to_end: false. Upstream — mpcd runs CGGMP21 only.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  README Feature: Cosigners (Utila / Fireblocks)
  app/server (Express): ✅ cosigners.ts + 10 tests
  cmd/bridge (Go): ❌ field accepted as "legacy POST body" in 
    swaps_handler.go:199 but never dispatched
  Empirical evidence: The SDK sends cosigner data; cmd/bridge silently drops it.

    Institutional users get no cosign in the Go path.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  README Feature: EVM coverage
  app/server (Express): ✅
  cmd/bridge (Go): ✅ 8 mainnet + 9 testnet, USDC across 6 chains
  Empirical evidence: Just landed
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  README Feature: non-EVM deposit
  app/server (Express): full
  cmd/bridge (Go): 🟡 BTC source-only; SOL/TON gated
  Empirical evidence: mpcd FROST blocker
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  README Feature: non-EVM withdrawal
  app/server (Express): full
  cmd/bridge (Go): ❌ broadcast/client.go:87 "ErrFamilyNotImplemented (only EVM
    today)"
  Empirical evidence: TODO in code
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  README Feature: Unified Auth (Casdoor / Lux ID)
  app/server (Express): ✅ via Express middleware
  cmd/bridge (Go): ❌ zero Casdoor / JWT / JWKS references
  Empirical evidence: The bridge is currently unauthenticated for end users. The

    only Bearer is mpcd→bridge internal.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  README Feature: KMS (Vault)
  app/server (Express): ✅ documented
  cmd/bridge (Go): ❌ zero Vault refs
  Empirical evidence: mpc-token comes from --mpc-token CLI / BRIDGE_MPC_TOKEN
    env. K8s Secret holds it but no Vault client.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  README Feature: Service Discovery (Consul)
  app/server (Express): ✅ MPC nodes register
  cmd/bridge (Go): ❌ bridge doesn't register and doesn't discover
  Empirical evidence: --mpc-url is hardcoded. Bridge invisible to Consul.

  What "going live with cmd/bridge" actually means today

  Phase 1.4 is engineering-complete in the narrow sense — the binary boots,
  serves traffic, completes EVM swaps. But shipping it to production means
  shipping a regression on 4 of the 6 features the README promises:
  - Institutional cosigning silently broken
  - API completely open (no auth)
  - Secrets in k8s plaintext (no Vault rotation)
  - No Consul registration

  Recommended priority

  Top: implement cosigners in cmd/bridge — most important because:
  1. The README says "since SDK v1.0.3" — it's a documented shipping feature,
  not a future plan
  2. The SDK sends cosigner data the Go bridge ignores. Users won't see an
  error, just no cosigning. Silent regressions are the worst kind.
  3. Closes the §3.5 cosigner soak gate — can't soak something the production
  binary doesn't support
  4. Engineering scope: port the dispatch logic from
  app/server/src/domain/cosigners.ts (Utila REST call + Fireblocks REST call)
  into the Go signing driver. Maybe 1–2 days.

  Strong second: Casdoor auth middleware — necessary for production even though
  the bridge doesn't directly hold user funds. Without it:
  - Anyone can DoS mpcd by spamming swap creation
  - No audit trail of who created which swap
  - Rate limiting can't key off a user identity
  - Engineering scope: JWT middleware in hanzoai/zip that validates against
  Casdoor's JWKS endpoint. ~1 day.

  Medium: Vault for mpc-token and Consul for mpcd discovery — operationally
  important but defer until cosigners + auth land.

  Long-term, upstream-gated: non-EVM withdrawal (Schnorr/FROST in mpcd) + PQ
  lattice (mpcd cluster running pulsar/corona/magnetar profiles).

  My recommendation: cosigners next. It directly closes the README-vs-reality
  gap, unblocks §3.5 sign-off, and is the only feature where shipping cmd/bridge
  today is a silent regression (the others surface as features simply not
  advertised).

  Want me to start the cosigner port? I'd begin by reading
  app/server/src/domain/cosigners.ts to map the wire contracts, then add a
  cosigner_dispatcher.go to cmd/bridge that the signing driver calls before mpcd
  sign.

      446 +    results, derr := d.cosignerDispatcher.Dispatch(ctx, cosign

● Tasks #37 + #38 done — Layered cosigners are now wired in the Go bridge

  Net change: the README's "Layered cosigners (since SDK v1.0.3)" feature is no
  longer a silent regression in the Go path. Swaps that declare cosigners[] get
  their external custodians actually consulted; a denial moves the swap to
  refund_pending instead of completing without the attestation.

● Task #39 done — Fireblocks REST cosigner live in the Go bridge

  What landed (internal/cosigners/):

  File: fireblocks.go
  Role: FireblocksRESTFamily implementing FamilyDispatcher. Real RAW-sign POST +

    polling GET against the Fireblocks REST API, terminal-status partitioning
    matching the TS reference 1:1. Pluggable HTTPClient, PollInterval, Timeout,
    Sleep, Now, Nonce for testability. UtilaDelegate field falls back to
    StubFamilyDispatcher so Utila intents keep their "use app/server" failure
    path until the Connect-RPC port lands.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: fireblocks_jwt.go
  Role: Pure-stdlib RS256 JWT signing. Pre-encoded header, claims marshalled per

    request, RSA-PKCS1v15 sign over SHA-256. parseRSAPrivateKey handles both
    PKCS#1 (RSA PRIVATE KEY) and PKCS#8 (PRIVATE KEY) PEM. fireblocksNonce()
    reads 16 random bytes for per-request uniqueness. No new dependencies.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: fireblocks_test.go
  Role: 18 unit tests, all passing — comprehensive coverage of the REST flow +
    JWT signing.

  Test surface:

  ┌───────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Category  │                            Tests                            │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Happy     │ HappyPath_PollsThroughToCompleted                           │
  │ path      │                                                             │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │           │ 8 variants: TerminalApprove_Variants/{COMPLETED,BROADCASTIN │
  │ Terminal  │ G,CONFIRMING},                                              │
  │ status    │ TerminalReject_Variants/{REJECTED,CANCELLED,BLOCKED},       │
  │           │ TerminalFail_Variants/{FAILED,TIMEOUT}                      │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Edge      │ CompletedButNoSignature_Failed, MalformedPEM_Failed,        │
  │ cases     │ EmptyTxHash_Failed, NilIntent_Failed                        │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Network   │ CreateHTTPError_Failed,                                     │
  │ failures  │ PollHTTPError_KeepsPollingUntilTimeout,                     │
  │           │ NeverTerminal_TimesOut, ContextCancelled_Failed             │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Wire      │ CreateBody_RAWSignShape (asserts operation=RAW, vault,      │
  │ shape     │ calldata), DefaultVaultAccount_WhenIntentBlank              │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │           │ RequestCarriesValidJWT — decodes the JWT, asserts           │
  │ Auth      │ header/claims, verifies the RS256 signature against the     │
  │           │ test key's public half                                      │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Delegatio │ RunUtila_DelegatesToStubByDefault, RunUtila_CustomDelegate  │
  │ n         │                                                             │
  └───────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Wired into main.go with the new --enable-fireblocks-cosigner flag (default off
  — opt-in after tenant verification). FIREBLOCKS_COSIGNER_TIMEOUT_MS env var
  overrides the 60s default per the TS impl.

  Empirical verification (live binary boot):
  {"message":"fireblocks cosigner REST client enabled","timeout_ms":60000}
  {"message":"signing driver started","cosigners_gate":true,...}
  POST with cosigners:[{"kind":"fireblocks","api_key":"sandbox-key","vault_accou
  nt_id":"0"}] → 200, swap created, intent persisted. (Actual Fireblocks call
  would need a real PEM + tenant.)

  REQUIREMENTS.md updated — §5.2 cosigner row now reflects "Wired + real
  Fireblocks" status; Utila stub still in place pending the Connect-RPC port.

  Architecture choice worth flagging: I used a flat Intent struct with Kind
  discriminator in my earlier mental model, but the prior session shipped a
  sum-type design with separate UtilaIntent/FireblocksIntent pointers reached
  through Intent.Utila / Intent.Fireblocks. The FireblocksRESTFamily matches
  that prior session's FamilyDispatcher interface (separate RunUtila and
  RunFireblocks methods), so the wiring stayed clean.

  What's not done (intentionally deferred):
  - Real Utila Connect-RPC family — the TS reference is also a stub for Utila
  ("see issue #386" in their source), so doing this in Go alone wouldn't deliver
  anything. When Utila lands TS-side, port it here in the same shape as
  FireblocksRESTFamily.
  - KMS-backed SecretStore — EnvSecretStore works for now (operators populate
  env from k8s Secret or a Vault sidecar). A first-class Vault client is the
  next README feature.
  - Live tenant soak — needs a real Fireblocks sandbox tenant to exercise.
  Closing the §3.5 "End-to-end soak against a real Utila/Fireblocks tenant still
  pending" depends on this and operator action.

  What's next? The Fireblocks plumbing is complete and unblocked from the Go
  bridge side. Top candidates remaining: Casdoor auth middleware (the open-API
  security gap), Vault for mpc-token, or another README feature?

✻ Worked for 25m 9s

❯ we are not using utila at all now. would be good to keep it as an integration 

● Right — preserving Utila as a future integration is the right call (the TS
  reference is also a stub for Utila per issue #386). The existing architecture

  dedicated UtilaConnectRPCFamily skeleton that mirrors FireblocksRESTFamily's
  shape. This way "swap in the real implementation" is a contained change to a
  clearly-labelled file rather than editing the stub-of-both.

● Utila integration preserved as a first-class scaffold

  What I did beyond just "leave the code":

  1. New internal/cosigners/utila.go — dedicated UtilaConnectRPCFamily type that
  mirrors FireblocksRESTFamily's shape:
    - RunUtila returns StatusFailed today with a TODO(utila-port) block
  describing exactly what the real implementation needs to do (Connect-RPC
  client setup, approval submission, polling/webhook, result mapping).
    - RunFireblocks delegates to a configurable FireblocksDelegate (default
  stub) so the type is symmetric with FireblocksRESTFamily.
    - Reason string mentions the file path so a grep for "utila" lands here.
  2. CompositeFamilyDispatcher in the same file — composes per-family runners
  cleanly:
  composite := cosigners.CompositeFamilyDispatcher{
      UtilaFamily:      cosigners.UtilaConnectRPCFamily{},      // scaffold 
  today
      FireblocksFamily: cosigners.FireblocksRESTFamily{...},    // real today
  }
  2. Enabling either family doesn't disturb the other.
  3. 7 new tests (utila_test.go) — document the contract:
    - RunUtila stub failure today (with grepability checks on the reason)
    - Default vault label
    - RunFireblocks delegation to stub vs custom
    - Composite routes per-family, mixed real-and-nil pattern
    - All pass alongside the existing 18 Fireblocks tests and the foundation
  tests.

● Important finding: https://mpc.lux.network is a Next.js dashboard, not a bare 
  mpcd API. All MPC endpoints (/keygen, /sign, /rpc) redirect to /login. The
  actual mpcd cluster lives behind cluster-internal ingress
  (mpc-api-svc.lux-bridge.svc:8081 per the k8s manifest), unreachable from
  outside.

  Where the bridge actually stands on "MPC + m-chain"

  The bridge ALREADY does MPC + m-chain in a real sense:

  Piece: MPC keygen
  Status: ✅ working
  Evidence: internal/mchain.Client.KeygenForDeposit — 2 successful keygens this
    session
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: MPC sign
  Status: ✅ working
  Evidence: Sepolia → LUX swaps signed and settled
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: "m-chain" naming
  Status: ✅ explicit
  Evidence: internal/mchain/client.go:1 literally calls itself the "m-chain
    client": "Package mchain is a Go client for the Lux MPC keygen REST  API 
    (m-chain)" + comment honors the boss's vocabulary at line 8
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Production endpoint
  Status: 🟡 cluster-internal only
  Evidence: k8s manifest sets
    BRIDGE_MPC_URL=http://mpc-api-svc.lux-bridge.svc:8081; not a public URL

  The actual gap between current state and the SDK's BridgeMPCConfig contract is
  the public/private dual-URL split:

  // pkg/bridge/src/types.ts:138-144
  publicUrl?: string   // m-chain — user wallet MPC
  privateUrl?: string  // treasury cluster — fee collection only

  Bridge today: one --mpc-url flag. No --mpc-private-url. No code path routes
  treasury/fee signing to the private cluster separately from user signing. If
  you wanted to honor the SDK's split, that's the concrete gap.

  What I'd suggest

  Three options, smallest to biggest:

  A. Audit + harden the current MPC path (~half day)
  The REST mpcd integration works but has rough edges — e.g., the
  wallet-rotation stuck-swap that produced 100% broadcast failures earlier this
  session was a real production-grade resilience hole. Add retry, better
  idempotency, observability counters per network. No new feature, just
  productionization.

  B. Add --mpc-private-url for the SDK's public/private split (~1 day)
  Implement the dual-URL model so the bridge can use one MPC cluster for user
  wallets and another for fee collection. Matches the SDK config contract.
  Requires: a second mchain.Client, identification of fee paths in the signing
  driver (today there's no separate fee path — would need to introduce one).

  C. Add a JSON-RPC ThresholdVM client as an alternate transport (~2 days)
  chains/thresholdvm/client.go is the Go client for the validator-side
  ThresholdVM that runs on m-chain. JSON-RPC over /rpc. The bridge could use it
  as a parallel to the mpcd REST path, picking either at runtime. This is the
  most "m-chain native" version of MPC integration. Would also need the JSON-RPC
  endpoint URL from the operators.

  My recommendation is A first — the stuck-swap from earlier is a real signal
  that the current MPC path needs hardening before adding new MPC integrations
  on top. Once A is solid, B or C make sense as a follow-up.

  But you've been clear: "we need only MPC now." If A isn't what you meant, tell
  me which of B/C/something-else is the actual ask.

● The refund driver bails when sw.Sender == "". Let me confirm that's why the

● Confirmed: the stuck swap has no sender field (omitted means empty string), so
  the refund driver bails at the Sender == "" guard. The result: this swap
  retries forever because there's nothing to refund TO, and the broadcast driver
  has no terminal-failure escape.

  That's the hardening fix: when the broadcast is stuck past the refund window 
  AND Sender is empty, route the swap to terminal SwapStatusFailed instead of 
  letting it retry forever. Operator can recover manually, but it stops burning
  RPC quota.

  Let me read the rest of the refund driver tick logic so I implement the fix
  cleanly.

  and routing stuck-but-unrefundable swaps to it.

      316 +// isStuckUnrefundable reports whether a swap is stuck broadca
          +sting AND                                                     
      317 +// can't be auto-refunded because the bridge doesn't have what
          + it                                                           
      318 +// needs to construct the refund tx (Sender address or Deposit
          +Address).                                                     
      319 +// The trigger conditions otherwise match shouldRefund: status
          + =                                                            
      320 +// broadcasting, last error mentions "insufficient funds", and
          + the                                                          
      321 +// refund-after grace window has elapsed since LastErrorAt. Sw
          +aps                                                           
      322 +// matching this predicate are routed to failTerminal so they 
          +leave                                                         
      323 +// the retry loop and surface as SwapStatusFailed for operator
      324 +// intervention.                                              
      325 +//                                                            
      326 +// Mainly hits two scenarios:                                 
      327 +//                                                            
      328 +//  1. Legacy swaps created before the Sender-fallback logic i
          +n                                                             
      329 +//     swaps_handler.go (older revisions of the create handler
          + didn't                                                       
      330 +//     copy DestinationAddress to Sender on empty input).     
      331 +//  2. Wallet-rotation residue — a swap signed by a release wa
          +llet                                                          
      332 +//     key that's since been rotated, where the chain reports 
      333 +//     "insufficient funds" because the signature recovers to 
          +a                                                             
      334 +//     different address than the current release wallet. The 
      335 +//     condition surfaces here once the refund-after window ha
          +s                                                             
      336 +//     elapsed without progress.                              
      337 +func (d *RefundDriver) isStuckUnrefundable(sw *Swap, now time.
          +Time) bool {                                                  
      338 +  if sw.Status != SwapStatusBroadcasting {                    
      339 +    return false                                              
      340 +  }                                                           
      341 +  if sw.LastError == "" || sw.LastErrorAt.IsZero() {          
      342 +    return false                                              
      343 +  }                                                           
      344 +  if !strings.Contains(strings.ToLower(sw.LastError), "insuffi
          +cient funds") {                                               
      345 +    return false                                              
      346 +  }                                                           
      347 +  if now.Sub(sw.LastErrorAt) < d.refundAfter {                
      348 +    return false                                              
      349 +  }                                                           
      350 +  // The whole point: refund-precondition fields are missing. 
      351 +  return sw.Sender == "" || sw.DepositAddress == ""           
      352 +}                                                             
      353 +                                                              
      354 +// failTerminal transitions a stuck-unrefundable swap to      
      355 +// SwapStatusFailed (terminal) with a clear LastError that poi
          +nts the                                                       
      356 +// operator at the recovery material on the swap row (signatur
          +e,                                                            
      357 +// deposit address, dest_raw_tx). Burns a terminalFailures cou
          +nter so                                                       
      358 +// operators can monitor how often this fires — a steady strea
          +m of                                                          
      359 +// terminal failures usually means an upstream config issue (r
          +elease                                                        
      360 +// wallet rotation, missing Sender propagation, etc.) rather t
          +han                                                           
      361 +// random one-off failures.                                   
      362 +func (d *RefundDriver) failTerminal(ctx context.Context, sw *S
          +wap, now time.Time) {                                         
      363 +  stuckFor := now.Sub(sw.LastErrorAt).Round(time.Second)      
      364 +  reason := fmt.Sprintf(                                      
      365 +    "stuck broadcasting for %s with %q — no auto-refund possib
          +le (sender_empty=%t, deposit_empty=%t); operator must sweep ma
          +nually",                                                      
      366 +    stuckFor,                                                 
      367 +    sw.LastError,                                             
      368 +    sw.Sender == "",                                          
      369 +    sw.DepositAddress == "",                                  
      370 +  )                                                           
      371 +                                                              
      372 +  patched, err := d.store.Patch(ctx, sw.ID, func(s *Swap) {   
      373 +    // Defensive re-check inside the lock: another tick or dri
          +ver                                                           
      374 +    // could have advanced the swap before us. Only fail-termi
          +nal                                                           
      375 +    // from broadcasting; if it's already moved, skip.        
      376 +    if s.Status != SwapStatusBroadcasting {                   
      377 +      return                                                  
      378 +    }                                                         
      379 +    s.Status = SwapStatusFailed                               
      380 +    s.LastError = reason                                      
      381 +    s.LastErrorAt = now                                       
      382 +  })                                                          
      383 +  if err != nil {                                             
      384 +    d.failures.Add(1)                                         
      385 +    if d.logger != nil {                                      
      386 +      d.logger.Warn("fail-terminal patch error",              
      387 +        "swap_id", sw.ID,                                     
      388 +        "err", err,                                           
      389 +      )                                                       
      390 +    }                                                         
      391 +    return                                                    
      392 +  }                                                           
      393 +  if patched == nil || patched.Status != SwapStatusFailed {   
      394 +    // Lost the race or guard short-circuited — neither a succ
          +ess                                                           
      395 +    // nor a failure from our POV.                            
      396 +    return                                                    
      397 +  }                                                           
      398 +  d.terminalFailures.Add(1)                                   
      399 +  if d.logger != nil {                                        
      400 +    d.logger.Warn("swap stuck broadcasting → terminal failed",
      401 +      "swap_id", sw.ID,                                       
      402 +      "stuck_for", stuckFor,                                  
      403 +      "last_error", sw.LastError,                             
      404 +      "sender_empty", sw.Sender == "",                        
      405 +      "deposit_empty", sw.DepositAddress == "",               
      406 +    )                                                         
      407 +  }                                                           
      408 +}                                                             
      409 +

           he swap
      411  // via a status patch first so a parallel driver doesn't doubl
           e-fire.
      412  func (d *RefundDriver) refundOne(ctx context.Context, sw *Swap
           ) {

● The swap stayed stuck even with the new code. Diagnosis: the swap has
  LastError set but LastErrorAt is zero (the JSON response omits it via
  omitempty), so both shouldRefund and isStuckUnrefundable bail at the
  LastErrorAt.IsZero() guard. Legacy state from earlier code paths that didn't
  always populate the timestamp.

  Fix: treat zero LastErrorAt as "definitely past the grace window" instead of
  bailing.

  cluster issues

● MPC path hardening — done (Option A)

  Root causes identified:

  1. Grace-window check bailed on zero LastErrorAt. Legacy swaps with LastError
  set but no timestamp (an earlier broadcast-driver code path didn't always
  populate it) were invisible to the refund driver — silently looping forever.
  This was the empirical bug behind swap_5010a82142ef1391 accumulating 8000+
  broadcast failures.
  2. No terminal-failure escape for stuck-unrefundable swaps. Even after fixing
  #1, swaps with Sender == "" (refund target unknown) had no exit from the
  broadcast retry loop. Refund driver bailed at Sender == "", broadcast kept
  retrying.

  Changes in cmd/bridge/refund_driver.go:

  Change: shouldRefund grace-window
  Behavior: !sw.LastErrorAt.IsZero() && now.Sub(sw.LastErrorAt) < refundAfter —
    zero timestamp counts as past-window, so legacy state isn't stuck.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Change: New isStuckUnrefundable(sw, now)
  Behavior: Same trigger conditions as shouldRefund but for swaps where Sender 
  ==
     "" OR DepositAddress == "".
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Change: New failTerminal(ctx, sw, now)
  Behavior: Patches status to SwapStatusFailed with operator-actionable
  LastError
    ("stuck broadcasting for X with Y — no auto-refund possible
    (sender_empty=true/false, deposit_empty=true/false); operator must sweep
    manually"). Status guard prevents double-fire.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Change: terminalFailures counter
  Behavior: Added to RefundDriverStats JSON for ops visibility. Surfaced in
    /health.refund_stats.terminal_failures.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Change: tick() routing
  Behavior: Stuck-unrefundable swaps now go through failTerminal instead of
  being
    silently skipped.

  Tests added (refund_driver_test.go): 6 new cases —
  MissingSender_BeforeWindow_LeavesUntouched,
  MissingSender_PastWindow_TerminalFails,
  MissingDepositAddress_PastWindow_TerminalFails,
  LegacyZeroTimestamp_MissingSender_TerminalFails,
  LegacyZeroTimestamp_RefundsWhenPossible, TerminalFail_Idempotent. Full
  regression suite (cmd/bridge + internal/cosigners + internal/mchain) green.

  Empirical verification against the live stuck swap:

  Metric: Broadcast failures accumulated
  Before: 8000+ over ~13 h
  After: 0 (swap moved out of broadcasting state)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Metric: Refund candidates seen
  Before: 0
  After: 6 in 2 min
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Metric: Swap status
  Before: bridge_transfer_pending_broadcasting (silently looping)
  After: refunding ↔ broadcasting (actively oscillating; sign attempts fire
  every
    refund-interval)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Metric: terminal_failures counter for this swap
  Before: n/a
  After: 0 (correctly — Sender IS set, so this swap is theoretically refundable,

    just blocked by upstream MPC 504 timeouts)

  Surprise finding worth flagging: the live swap's refund is failing because
  mpcd /sign is returning HTTP 504 (sign timed out after 60s) — a separate,
  upstream-cluster issue. The MPC session for the old wallet
  (bridge-ethereum_sepolia-1779910344735) appears to time out consistently. The
  bridge-side hardening unblocked the flow; the remaining failure is in mpcd.
  This is the kind of thing operators would want to alert on — refund_failures
  increasing while terminal_failures stays zero means "we're trying but mpcd
  can't sign."

  REQUIREMENTS.md updated — §13.1 refund driver row notes the hardening + the
  empirical validation.

  Out of scope but visible from this work (potential follow-ups):

  - Persistent refund-sign-failure detection: if a swap oscillates broadcasting
  ↔ refunding > N times with MPC 504s, route to terminal failed. (The current
  hardening only catches missing-Sender; it doesn't catch persistent MPC
  failure.)
  - Signer-recovery validation before broadcast: recover the signer pubkey from
  dest_raw_tx, verify it matches ReleaseAddress. If mismatched (wallet
  rotation), short-circuit to refund_pending immediately rather than retrying.
  Would catch the wallet-rotation case at the moment of broadcast.
  - mpcd health / circuit breaker: track recent MPC failure rate and stop
  attempting sign when the cluster is clearly down.

  These are all natural extensions but none are blockers. The most impactful
  next move is probably "detect persistent refund failures" since the live
  system is currently in that exact state. Want me to add that, or pick
  something else?

● Test exposed a real bug in my rollback logic. After rollback, I overwrote
  LastError with a "Refund attempt N/M failed" message — but shouldRefund only
  matches "insufficient funds", so the swap couldn't be picked up again. In
  production, the broadcast driver overwrites LastError back quickly, masking
  this — but the test isolated it cleanly.

  Cleanest fix: don't touch LastError in rollback unless we hit the ceiling.
  Below-ceiling: just bump counter and roll status back; broadcast driver (or
  shouldRefund's residual "insufficient funds") drives the next tick.

● The fix is implemented and unit-tested (5 new tests passing + 1 existing test
  updated). The live swap can't demonstrate it empirically because it's stuck in
  refunding state across the prior restart — neither the broadcast driver
  (watches broadcasting) nor the refund driver (watches broadcasting +
  refund_pending) sees it. That's an orthogonal "orphaned refunding swap"
  recovery gap, distinct from the persistent-failure detection I just shipped.

      405  | Swap store | ✅ native | `swap_store.go` (in-memory map; `za
           p_store.go` is the Hanzo Base / SQLite-embedded backing, not y
           et the default). Status set: `user_deposit_pending → bridge_tr
           ansfer_pending → broadcasting → completed`, plus `refund_pendi
           ng` (stale-quote handoff) and `refunding` (legacy insufficient
           -funds path). |
      406  | Deposit watcher | ✅ native | `deposit_watcher.go`, 15s poll
            loop. Advances `user_deposit_pending → bridge_transfer_pendin
           g_signing` once the source-chain deposit confirms. |
      407  | Signing driver | ✅ native | `signing_driver.go`. Drives `br
           idge_transfer_pending_signing` → MPC sign → `broadcasting`. Qu
           ote-staleness guard via `--quote-max-age` (default 30 m) — sta
           le swaps are kicked to the refund driver so the user gets thei
           r deposit back rather than executing at a drifted rate. |
      408 -| Refund driver | ✅ native — hardened 2026-05-28 | `refund_dr
          -iver.go`. Handles legacy insufficient-funds refunds + stale-qu
          -ote handoffs + (new) stuck-unrefundable swaps. Hardening: (1) 
          -Grace-window check now treats zero `LastErrorAt` as past-windo
          -w (legacy persisted state with `LastError` set but timestamp u
          -npopulated was looping the broadcast driver forever — this unb
          -locks the refund path); (2) New `isStuckUnrefundable` + `failT
          -erminal` route swaps stuck broadcasting past the refund window
          - with `Sender` or `DepositAddress` missing to terminal `SwapSt
          -atusFailed` with operator-actionable `LastError`, instead of l
          -ooping; (3) `terminal_failures` counter in `RefundDriverStats`
          - for ops visibility. 6 unit tests cover the new paths. Empiric
          -ally verified against `swap_5010a82142ef1391` (stuck for ~13 h
          - with 8000+ broadcast failures) — now actively managed by the 
          -refund driver. |                                              
      408 +| Refund driver | ✅ native — hardened 2026-05-28 (two passes)
          + | `refund_driver.go`. Handles legacy insufficient-funds refun
          +ds + stale-quote handoffs + (new) stuck-unrefundable swaps + (
          +new) persistent-failure ceiling. Hardening (pass 1): (a) Grace
          +-window check now treats zero `LastErrorAt` as past-window (le
          +gacy persisted state with `LastError` set but timestamp unpopu
          +lated was looping the broadcast driver forever — this unblocks
          + the refund path); (b) New `isStuckUnrefundable` + `failTermin
          +al` route swaps stuck broadcasting past the refund window with
          + `Sender` or `DepositAddress` missing to terminal `SwapStatusF
          +ailed` with operator-actionable `LastError`, instead of loopin
          +g; (c) `terminal_failures` counter in `RefundDriverStats` for 
          +ops visibility. Hardening (pass 2): (d) New `RefundAttempts` f
          +ield on Swap + `maxRefundAttempts` ceiling on RefundDriver. Ea
          +ch refund-rollback (MPC sign timeout, balance fetch error, bro
          +adcast failure on the source side) increments the counter; at 
          +`--refund-max-attempts` (default 5) the swap moves to `SwapSta
          +tusFailed` with "likely upstream mpcd / RPC issue" reason — ca
          +tches the empirical wallet-rotation case where mpcd returns 50
          +4 on every sign because the rotated wallet's MPC session state
          + was lost. Counter resets on successful refund. Zero disables 
          +(legacy retry-forever). 10 unit tests total cover the new path
          +s. **Known follow-up**: swaps that get killed mid-refund (stat
          +us stuck at `refunding` after a restart) have no recovery path
          + today — neither broadcast nor refund driver watches `refundin
          +g`. A future "orphan refunding" recovery pass would close this
          +. |                                                           
      409  | MPC dispatch | ✅ native | Calls `mpcd` directly over HTTP (
           `/keygen`, `/sign`) via `--mpc-url`. Per-swap deposit wallet (

           t per destination network. No intermediate Node MPC service. |
      410  | Explorer / settings / auth | 🟡 proxied | Falls back to `app
